{"lesson":{"id":3748,"taskId":14193033,"topic":{"id":344,"name":"Further Logarithmic Differentiation"},"status":"Accepted","steps":[{"id":37035,"number":1,"type":"tutorial","contentId":6088,"tutorial":{"id":6088,"title":"Introduction","content":"When a function [math]y=y(x)[/math] contains complex products, quotients, or radicals, we can use logarithmic differentiation to find the derivative [math]y'(x).[/math] \n\nFor example, consider the following function: \n\n\\[\ny = x^2 \\cdot 2^x\n\\] \n\nOur goal is to find [math]y'(x).[/math] To do this, we will use the product rule for logarithms, namely\n\\[\n\\ln(a b)= \\ln a + \\ln b.\n\\] \n\nWe differentiate our function by following four steps.\n\n**Step 1.** We take the natural logarithm of both sides and apply the product and power rules:\n\n\\begin{align*}\n\\ln y & = \\ln \\left( x^2 \\cdot 2^x \\right) \\\\[5pt]\n\\ln y &= \\ln \\left( x^2 \\right) + \\ln \\left(2^x \\right) \\\\[5pt]\n\\ln y &= 2\\ln{x} + x \\ln2\n\\end{align*}\n\n**Step 2.** Next, we differentiate both sides of our equation using implicit differentiation:\n\n\\begin{align*}\n\\dfrac{\\textrm{d}}{\\textrm{d}x} \\left(\\ln y \\right) & = \\dfrac{\\textrm{d}}{\\textrm{d}x} \\left( 2\\ln{x} + x \\ln2 \\right) \\\\[5pt]\n\\dfrac{1}{y} \\cdot y' &= 2\\cdot(\\ln x)' + ({x})' \\cdot \\ln (2)    \\\\[5pt]\n\\dfrac{1}{y} \\cdot y' &= 2\\cdot \\dfrac 1x+ 1 \\cdot \\ln (2)    \\\\[5pt]\n\\dfrac{y'}{y} &= \\dfrac {2}{x} + \\ln 2 \\\\[5pt]\n\\end{align*}\n\n**Step 3.** Then, we solve for [math]y'{:}[/math]\n\n\\begin{align*}\ny' &= y \\left(\\dfrac {2}{x} + \\ln 2  \\right)\n\\end{align*}\n\n\n**Step 4.** Finally, we substitute back our original expression for [math]y =x^2\\cdot 2^x{:}[/math]\n\n\\begin{align*}\ny' \n\n&= x^2\\cdot 2^x \\left( \\dfrac {2}{x} + \\ln 2 \\right) \\\\[5pt]\n\n&= x\\cdot 2^{x+1} +  x^2\\cdot 2^x\\ln 2\n\n\n\\end{align*}\n\n\n\n","html":"<html><head><style type=\"text/css\">\n                            .mjpage .MJX-monospace {\n                            font-family: product rule for logarithms:\n\n\\begin{align*}\n\\ln y & = \\ln \\left( x^5 (\\cos x)^x \\right) \\\\[3pt]\n\\ln y &= \\ln \\left( x^5 \\right) + \\ln \\left( (\\cos x)^x \\right) \\\\[3pt]\n\\ln y &= 5\\ln{x} + x \\ln \\left( \\cos x \\right)\n\\end{align*}\n\nNext, we differentiate both sides with respect to [math]x{:}[/math]\n\n\\begin{align*}\n\\dfrac{\\textrm{d}}{\\textrm{d}x} \\left(\\ln y \\right) & = \\dfrac{\\textrm{d}}{\\textrm{d}x} \\left( 5\\ln{x} + x \\ln ( \\cos x ) \\right) \\\\[5pt]\n\\dfrac{1}{y} \\cdot y' &= \\dfrac {5}{x} + (x)' \\cdot \\ln ( \\cos x ) + x \\cdot \\left( \\ln ( \\cos x) \\right)'    \\\\[5pt]\n\\dfrac{y'}{y} &= \\dfrac {5}{x} + \\ln ( \\cos x ) + x \\cdot \\dfrac{1}{\\cos x} \\cdot (-\\sin x) \\\\[5pt]\n\\dfrac{y'}{y} &= \\dfrac {5}{x} + \\ln ( \\cos  x ) - x \\tan x\n\\end{align*}\n\nNow, we solve for [math]y'{:}[/math]\n\n\\begin{align*}\ny' &= y \\left( \\dfrac {5}{x} + \\ln ( \\cos  x ) - x \\tan x \\right)\n\\end{align*}\n\nFinally, we substitute [math]y = x^5 (\\cos x)^x {:}[/math]\n\n\\begin{align*}\ny' \n\n&= x^5 (\\cos x)^x \\left( \\dfrac {5}{x} + \\ln ( \\cos  x ) - x \\tan x \\right) \\\\[5pt]\n\n&= x^5 (\\cos x)^x \\left( \\dfrac {5}{x} + \\dfrac{x \\ln ( \\cos  x )}{x} - \\dfrac{x^2 \\tan x}{x} \\right) \\\\[5pt]\n\n&= x^4 (\\cos x)^x \\left( 5 + \\bbox[2pt,Gainsboro]{x \\ln ( \\cos  x ) - x^2 \\tan x} \\right)\n\\end{align*}\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\ng(x) = x \\ln ( \\cos  x ) - x^2 \\tan x. \n\\]","explanationHtml":"<html><head><style type=\"text/css\">\n                            .mjpage Logarithms to Differentiate a Quotient","content":"In cases where we wish to differentiate a quotient of functions\n\\[\ny =  f(x) = \\dfrac{g(x)}{h(x)},\n\\]\nwe can apply the quotient rule for logarithms: \n\n\\[\n\\ln \\left(\\dfrac{a}{b}\\right) = \\ln a - \\ln b\n\\] \n\nFor example, let's compute [math]y',[/math] where the function [math]y[/math] is given by\n\n\\[\ny = \\dfrac {e^{x^2}}{(1-2x)^2(x-1)^3}.\n\\]\n\nFirst, we find the natural logarithm of both sides and use the quotient and power rules for logarithms:\n\n\\begin{align*}\n\\ln y & = \\ln\\bigg(\\dfrac {e^{x^2}}{(1-2x)^2(x-1)^3} \\bigg)\\\\[5pt] \n\\ln y &= \\ln \\left( e^{x^2} \\right) - \\ln \\left( (1-2x)^2 \\right) - \\ln \\left( (x-1)^3 \\right) \\\\[5pt]\n\\ln y &= x^2 - 2\\ln(1-2x) - 3\\ln(x-1) \n\\end{align*}\n\nNext, we differentiate both sides with respect to [math]x{:}[/math]\n\n\\begin{align*}\n\\dfrac{\\textrm{d}}{\\textrm{d}x} \\left(\\ln y \\right) & = \\dfrac{\\textrm{d}}{\\textrm{d}x} \\left(x^2 - 2\\ln(1-2x) - 3\\ln(x-1)\\right) \\\\[5pt]\n\\dfrac{1}{y} \\cdot y' &= 2x - 2 \\cdot \\dfrac {1}{1-2x}\\cdot (-2) - 3 \\cdot \\dfrac {1}{x-1} \\\\[5pt]\n\\dfrac{y'}{y} &= 2x+\\dfrac {4}{1-2x} - \\dfrac {3}{x-1}\n\\end{align*}\n\nNow, we solve for [math]y'{:}[/math]\n\n\\begin{align*}\ny' &= y \\left( 2x+\\dfrac {4}{1-2x} - \\dfrac {3}{x-1} \\right)\n\\end{align*}\n\nFinally, we substitute our expression for [math]y,[/math] and simplify:\n\n\\begin{align*}\ny' \n&=  \\dfrac {e^{x^2}}{(1-2x)^2(x-1)^3} \\left(  2x+\\dfrac {4}{1-2x} - \\dfrac {3}{x-1}\\right)  \\\\[5pt]\n&=  \\dfrac {e^{x^2}}{(1-2x)^2(x-1)^3} \\left(  \\dfrac{2x(1-2x)(x-1)+4(x-1) - 3(1-2x)}{(1-2x)(x-1)}\\right)  \\\\[5pt]\n&=  \\dfrac {e^{x^2}}{(1-2x)^3(x-1)^4} \\Big(  2x(1-2x)(x-1)+4(x-1) - 3(1-2x)\\Big)  \\\\[5pt]\n&=  \\dfrac {e^{x^2}}{(1-2x)^3(x-1)^4} \\Big(  -4x^3+6x^2+8x-7\\Big)  \\\\[5pt]\n&=  -\\dfrac {e^{x^2}}{(1-2x)^3(x-1)^4} \\Big( 4x^3-6x^2-8x+7\\Big) \\\\[5pt]\n&=  -\\dfrac {e^{x^2}\\left( 4x^3-6x^2-8x+7\\right)}{(1-2x)^3(x-1)^4}  \\\\[5pt]\n\n\n\n\\end{align*}","html":"<html><head><style type=\"text/css\">\n use the quotient and power rules for logarithms:\n\n\\begin{align*}\n\\ln y & = \\ln \\left(\\dfrac{(x+3)^3}{(x+5)^6}\\right) \\\\[5pt] \n\\ln y &= \\ln \\left( (x+3)^3 \\right)  - \\ln \\left( (x+5)^6 \\right) \\\\[5pt]  \n\\ln y &= 3 \\ln(x+3) - 6\\ln(x+5)  \n\\end{align*}\n\nNext, we differentiate both sides with respect to [math]x{:}[/math]\n\n\\begin{align*}\n\\dfrac{\\textrm{d}}{\\textrm{d}x} \\left(\\ln y \\right) & = \\dfrac{\\textrm{d}}{\\textrm{d}x} \\left( 3 \\ln(x+3) - 6\\ln(x+5)  \\right) \\\\[5pt] \n\\dfrac{1}{y} \\cdot y' &= 3 \\cdot \\dfrac {1}{x+3} - 6 \\cdot \\dfrac {1}{x+5}  \\\\[5pt] \n\\dfrac{y'}{y} &= \\dfrac {3}{x+3} - \\dfrac {6}{x+5} \n\\end{align*} \n\nNow, we solve for [math]y'{:}[/math]\n\\begin{align*}\ny' &= y \\left( \\dfrac {3}{x+3} - \\dfrac {6}{x+5}  \\right)\n\\end{align*}\n\nFinally, we substitute [math]y =  \\dfrac{(x+3)^3}{(x+5)^6}{:}[/math] \n\n\\begin{align*}\ny' &=  \\dfrac{(x+3)^3}{(x+5)^6} \\left( \\dfrac {3}{x+3} - \\dfrac {6}{x+5}  \\right)  \\\\[5pt]\n\n&=  \\dfrac{(x+3)^3}{(x+5)^6} \\left( \\dfrac {3(x+5) - 6(x+3)}{(x+3)(x+5)} \\right)  \\\\[5pt]\n\n&=  \\dfrac{(x+3)^3}{(x+5)^6} \\left( \\dfrac {-3x-3}{(x+3)(x+5)} \\right)  \\\\[5pt]\n\n&=  -\\dfrac{\\bbox[2pt,Gainsboro]{3(x+1)}(x+3)^2}{(x+5)^7} \n\\end{align*} \n\nTherefore,\n\\[\ng(x) = \\bbox[2pt,Gainsboro]{3(x+1)}.\n\\]\n\n","explanationHtml":"<html><head><style type=\"text/css\">\n                            .mjpage .MJX-monospace {\n id=\"E3-MJMATHI-67\" d=\"M311 43Q296 30 267 15T206 0Q143 0 105 45T66 160Q66 265 143 353T314 442Q361 442 401 394L404 398Q406 401 409 404T418 412T431 419T447 422Q461 422 470 413T480 394Q480 379 423 function can be written as\n\n\\begin{align*}\ny \n&=  \\sqrt[3]{\\dfrac{x^2}{x^5+ 1}} \\\\[5pt]\n&=  \\dfrac{\\sqrt[3]{x^2}}{\\sqrt[3]{x^5+ 1}} \\\\[5pt]\n\n&= \\dfrac{x^{2/3}}{ (x^5+1)^{1/3}}.    \n\\end{align*}\n\nFirst, we take the natural logarithm of both sides and use the quotient and power rules for logarithms:\n\n\\begin{align*}\n\\ln y & = \\ln \\left(  \\dfrac{x^{2/3}}{ (x^5+1)^{1/3}}\\right) \\\\[5pt]  \n\\ln y &= \\ln \\left( x^{2/3} \\right) - \\ln \\left( (x^5+1)^{1/3} \\right) \\\\[5pt]   \n\\ln y &= \\dfrac{2}{3} \\ln x -\\dfrac{1}{3} \\ln (x^5 + 1 )    \n\\end{align*}\n \nNext, we differentiate both sides with respect to [math]x{:}[/math] \n\n\\begin{align*}\n\\dfrac{\\textrm{d}}{\\textrm{d}x} \\left(\\ln y \\right) & = \\dfrac{\\textrm{d}}{\\textrm{d}x} \\left( \\dfrac{2}{3} \\ln x -\\dfrac{1}{3} \\ln (x^5 + 1 )  \\right) \\\\[5pt]  \n\\dfrac{1}{y} \\cdot y' &= \\dfrac {2}{3} \\cdot \\dfrac {1}{x}  -\\dfrac {1}{3} \\cdot  \\dfrac {1}{x^5 + 1} \\cdot (5x^4) \\\\[5pt]    \n\\dfrac{y'}{y} &= \\dfrac{1}{3} \\left(\\dfrac {2}{x} - \\dfrac {5x^4}{x^5 + 1}  \\right)   \n\\end{align*} \n\nNow, we solve for [math]y'{:}[/math]\n\n\\begin{align*}\ny' &= y \\left(  \\dfrac{1}{3} \\left(\\dfrac {2}{x} - \\dfrac {5x^4}{x^5 + 1}  \\right)  \\right)     \n\\end{align*}\n\nFinally, we substitute [math]y =  \\sqrt[3]{\\dfrac{x^2}{x^5+ 1}}{:}[/math]\n\n\\begin{align*}\ny' &=  \\dfrac{1}{3} \\sqrt[3]{\\dfrac{x^2}{x^5+ 1}}  \\left(\\dfrac {2}{x} - \\dfrac {5x^4}{x^5 + 1}  \\right)  \\\\[5pt]\n\n&=  \\dfrac{1}{3} \\sqrt[3]{\\dfrac{x^2}{x^5+ 1}}  \\left(\\dfrac {2(x^5+1) - 5x^5}{x(x^5+1)}  \\right)  \\\\[5pt]\n\n&=  \\dfrac{1}{3} \\sqrt[3]{\\dfrac{x^2}{x^5+ 1}}  \\left(\\dfrac {-3x^5+2}{x(x^5+1)}  \\right)  \\\\[5pt]\n\n&=  \\dfrac{1}{3} \\sqrt[3]{\\dfrac{x^2}{x^5+ 1}}  \\left(\\dfrac {-3x^5+2}{\\sqrt[3]{x^3(x^5+1)^3}}  \\right)  \\\\[5pt]\n\n&= \\dfrac{\\bbox[2pt,Gainsboro]{-3x^5+2}}{3\\sqrt[3]{x(x^5+1)^4}}\n\\end{align*} \n\n\nTherefore,\n\\[\ng(x) = \\bbox[2pt,Gainsboro]{-3x^5+2}.\n\\]\n\n","explanationHtml":"<html><head><style
